Continuing work from thread T-019b368e-76e6-73f0-9bdc-55889e4a801a. When you lack specific information you can use read_thread to get it.

@src/editable/mod.rs @src/editable/state.rs @src/editable/messages.rs @src/update/text_edit.rs @src/update/csv.rs @src/update/ui.rs @src/messages.rs @src/runtime/input.rs @AGENTS.md

Other relevant files: docs/feature/unified-text-editing-final.md, docs/CHANGELOG.md

I recently completed the unified text editing system. This involved implementing a bridge in src/update/text_edit.rs to map TextEditMsg variants to existing EditorMsg/DocumentMsg for the main editor. I also added full clipboard integration (copy, cut, paste) and selection movement (Shift+Arrow, Shift+Home/End, Shift+Option+Arrow) for both modal inputs and CSV cell editing. This required updating src/messages.rs with new CsvMsg and ModalMsg variants, implementing the corresponding logic in src/update/csv.rs and src/update/ui.rs, and wiring up the keyboard shortcuts in src/runtime/input.rs. I also performed a polish sweep, updating documentation like CHANGELOG.md, README.md, and AGENTS.md, and cleaning up some minor code issues.

The core unified text editing system is now complete, and all previously identified milestones are marked as done.

However, there are still some known implementation gaps and stubbed methods:
- In src/update/text_edit.rs, the TextEditMsg::SelectWord variant is currently a no-op with a TODO comment.
- In src/update/ui.rs, the ModalState::FindReplace confirm action has a TODO to implement the actual find/replace execution.
- The bridge in src/update/text_edit.rs for TextEditMsg::MoveLineUp and TextEditMsg::MoveLineDown currently returns None, meaning these operations are not yet supported for the main editor via the unified system.
- There are other general TODOs in the codebase, such as implementing pinned tab functionality, preview tab behavior, and persisting window size/position.

You also pointed out that the '-' and '+' characters in the sidebar need a space after the symbol to look right, which is a UI polish item.

Key patterns and libraries I've been using include:
- EditableState for managing text buffer, cursor, selection, and undo/redo.
- StringBuffer for single-line inputs (used in modals and CSV cells).
- TextEditMsg and EditContext for a unified message system.
- arboard for clipboard access.
- The bridge pattern to integrate new TextEditMsg with existing EditorMsg/DocumentMsg handlers.

  "task": "Analyze why adding a space to the folder indicator string doesn't create visual spacing between the +/- and the folder name.   ],

## User

**Tool Result:** `toolu_vrtx_01L3Pds5LyyCQw4Z7xXqdKgD`

```
1) TL;DR
Adding a space to the indicator string doesn’t move the folder name because the name is always drawn at a fixed `text_x = x_offset + 16`, independent of how wide the indicator text is. The fix is to change how `text_x` is computed (or the constant `16`) so it accounts for the indicator’s width instead of relying on spaces.

---

2) Recommended approach (simple path)

Right now:

```rust
let icon_x = x_offset;
let text_x = x_offset + 16; // Space for chevron indicator
...
if node.is_dir {
    let is_expanded = workspace.is_expanded(&node.path);
    let indicator = if is_expanded { "- " } else { "+ " };
    painter.draw(frame, icon_x, text_y, indicator, icon_color);
}
...
painter.draw(frame, text_x, text_y, &display_name, fg);
```

`text_x` is hard-coded to `x_offset + 16`, so changing `indicator` from `"+"` to `"+ "` only affects what’s drawn inside that 16‑px region; it never changes where the name starts.

The simplest correct fix is to compute `text_x` based on the indicator’s pixel width plus a padding, using the `char_width` you already have for the font.

Example adjustment:

1. Use the `char_width` argument in `render_sidebar` (remove the underscore) and pass it down into `render_node`.

```rust
fn render_sidebar(
    frame: &mut Frame,
    painter: &mut TextPainter,
    model: &AppModel,
    sidebar_width: usize,
    sidebar_height: usize,
    _line_height: usize,
    char_width: f32,  // <- use this
) {
    ...
    fn render_node(
        frame: &mut Frame,
        painter: &mut TextPainter,
        node: &token::model::FileNode,
        workspace: &token::model::Workspace,
        y: &mut usize,
        visible_index: &mut usize,
        depth: usize,
        sidebar_width: usize,
        row_height: usize,
        indent: f32,
        text_color: u32,
        selection_bg: u32,
        selection_fg: u32,
        folder_icon_color: u32,
        sidebar_height: usize,
        scroll_offset: usize,
        char_width: f32, // <- new param
    ) {
        ...
```

2. When you call `render_node`, pass `char_width`.

```rust
render_node(
    frame,
    painter,
    &workspace.root,
    workspace,
    &mut y,
    &mut visible_index,
    0,
    sidebar_width,
    row_height,
    indent,
    text_color,
    selection_bg,
    selection_fg,
    folder_icon_color,
    sidebar_height,
    scroll_offset,
    char_width,
);
```

3. Inside `render_node`, compute `text_x` from the indicator width:

```rust
let x_offset = (depth as f32 * indent) as usize + 8; // 8px left padding
let icon_x = x_offset;

// Default: no indicator
let mut indicator = "";
let mut indicator_color = text_color;

// Directories: draw +/- and reserve space equal to its width
if node.is_dir {
    let is_expanded = workspace.is_expanded(&node.path);
    indicator = if is_expanded { "-" } else { "+" }; // no need for a trailing space
    indicator_color = if is_selected { selection_fg } else { folder_icon_color };
    painter.draw(frame, icon_x, text_y, indicator, indicator_color);
}

// Compute the indicator pixel width and some gap after it
let indicator_chars = indicator.chars().count() as f32;
let indicator_width = (indicator_chars * char_width).round() as usize;
let gap = (char_width / 2.0).round() as usize; // visual padding between +/- and name

let text_x = icon_x + indicator_width + gap;
```

Then leave the display name drawing as-is:

```rust
painter.draw(frame, text_x, text_y, &display_name, fg);
```

Effort: S (well under 1h).

---

3) Rationale and trade-offs

- Why the space didn’t help:
  The space character is rendered *inside* the same fixed 16‑px icon area; it doesn’t change the separately calculated `text_x`. Visually, you’re just deciding how much of that 16‑px slot is used by the indicator glyph vs. blank, but the folder name always starts at the same pixel.
- Why compute from `char_width`:
  It keeps the layout tied to the actual font metrics instead of a magic `16` constant. If you change font size or DPI, the spacing still looks correct.
- Simplicity:
  This uses an existing parameter (`char_width`) and a small local change; no new types or complex layout logic.

Alternative (even simpler, but more “magic”): just bump the constant:

```rust
let text_x = x_offset + 20; // or 24, etc.
```

This works but stays tied to a hard-coded pixel value and may not scale nicely with font size.

---

4) Risks and guardrails

- If you mis-thread `char_width` into `render_node`, you’ll get a compile error (extra/missing param), which is straightforward to fix.
- Make sure you only use `indicator_width` > 0 for directories; for files, the indicator string should be empty so `indicator_width` is 0 and `text_x` is just `icon_x + gap`.
- Test with different scale factors / DPI to confirm spacing feels reasonable; you may tweak `gap` (e.g., `0.5 * char_width` vs `1.0 * char_width`).

---

5) When to consider the advanced path

- If you add real icon glyphs or images instead of text `+`/`-`.
- If you support variable-width fonts in the sidebar.
- If you want ellipsis/truncation to be pixel-perfect rather than estimated by `estimated_char_width`.

---

6) Optional advanced path

Instead of approximating `indicator_width` from `char_width`, use the actual font metrics (via your `TextPainter` or `fontdue` metrics) to measure the indicator string’s rendered width in pixels and base `text_x` on that measurement. That’s slightly more work and only worth it if you move away from strict monospace assumptions or start mixing icons and text.
```

          "text": "Continuing work from thread T-019b239e-0f2f-71ce-b793-63a784c6aa4f. When you lack specific information you can use read_thread to get it.\n\nI just finished implementing syntax highlighting for all Phase 3, 4, and 5 languages as outlined in the roadmap. This involved:\n- Adding new tree-sitter grammar dependencies to Cargo.toml.\n- Updating the LanguageId enum in src/syntax/languages.rs, including its from_extension() and display_name() methods.\n- Creating new .scm query files in the queries/ directory for TypeScript, JSON, and TOML.\n- Using the built-in HIGHLIGHTS_QUERY or HIGHLIGHT_QUERY for Python, Go, PHP, C, C++, Java, and Bash.\n- Registering all new languages in the ParserState::init_language() function in src/syntax/parser.rs.\n- Adding dedicated query compilation tests and basic parsing tests for each new language in src/syntax/parser.rs.\n- Upgrading the core tree-sitter dependency from 0.24 to 0.25 to resolve ABI compatibility issues with newer grammar crates.\n- Updating docs/ROADMAP.md and docs/CHANGELOG.md to reflect these changes.\n- I also created comprehensive sample files for each of the 17 supported languages in the samples/syntax/ directory.\n\nThe current syntax highlighting system runs in a background worker thread with a 30ms debounce. It uses tree-sitter for parsing and query-based highlighting. Queries are embedded using include_str!().\n\nFor the next task, I need to add a Makefile target to open all these sample files for manual testing.",
          "type": "text"
        }
      ]

  "content": "1: # Changelog\n2: \n3: All notable changes to rust-editor are documented in this file.\n4: \n5: ---\n6: \n7: ## v0.3.8 - 2025-12-19 (Latest)\n8: \n9: ### Added - Unified Text Editing System\n10: \n11: Major refactoring to unify text editing across all input contexts (modals, CSV cells) with consistent behavior:\n12: \n13: **Core Architecture (`src/editable/` module):**\n14: - **`TextBuffer` / `TextBufferMut` traits** - Abstract over String and Rope buffer backends\n15: - **`StringBuffer`** - Efficient single-line buffer for modals and CSV cells\n16: - **`EditableState<B>`** - Unified state container with cursor, selection, and undo history\n17: - **`EditConstraints`** - Context-specific restrictions (multiline, multi-cursor, char filters)\n18: - **`TextEditMsg` / `MoveTarget`** - Unified message types for all editing operations\n19: - **`EditContext`** - Identifies which input area is being edited\n20: - **`TextFieldRenderer`** - Unified text field rendering with selection support\n21: \n22: **Modal Input Improvements:**\n23: - Full cursor navigation in all modals (Left/Right, Home/End)\n24: - Selection support (Shift+Arrow) in command palette, goto line, find/replace\n25: - Word movement (Option+Arrow) in all modals\n26: - Word deletion (Option+Backspace/Delete) in all modals\n27: - Select all (Cmd+A) in all modals\n28: - Undo/redo within modal inputs\n29: - Delete forward (Delete key) now works in modals\n30: - Clipboard integration (Cmd+C/X/V) in all modals\n31: \n32: **CSV Cell Editor Enhancements:**\n33: - Migrated `CellEditState` to use `EditableState<StringBuffer>`\n34: - Word movement (Option+Left/Right) while editing cells\n35: - Word deletion (Option+Backspace/Delete) while editing cells\n36: - Select all (Cmd+A) while editing cells\n37: - Undo/redo (Cmd+Z / Cmd+Shift+Z) within cell editing session\n38: - Selection support (Shift+Arrow, Shift+Home/End, Shift+Option+Arrow) while editing cells\n39: - Clipboard integration (Cmd+C/X/V) while editing cells\n40: \n41: **New Messages:**\n42: - `CsvMsg::EditCursorWordLeft`, `EditCursorWordRight`\n43: - `CsvMsg::EditDeleteWordBackward`, `EditDeleteWordForward`\n44: - `CsvMsg::EditSelectAll`, `EditUndo`, `EditRedo`\n45: - `CsvMsg::EditCursorLeftWithSelection`, `EditCursorRightWithSelection`, etc. - Selection movement\n46: - `CsvMsg::EditCopy`, `EditCut`, `EditPaste` - Clipboard operations\n47: - `ModalMsg::Copy`, `Cut`, `Paste` - Modal clipboard operations\n48: - `Msg::TextEdit(EditContext, TextEditMsg)` - Unified text editing dispatch\n49: \n50: **Main Editor Bridge:**\n51: - `bridge_text_edit_to_editor()` maps `TextEditMsg` to legacy `EditorMsg`/`DocumentMsg`\n52: - Enables unified message system to control main editor via bridge pattern\n53: - All movement, selection, editing, clipboard, undo/redo, and multi-cursor operations bridged\n54: - Allows gradual migration without breaking existing functionality\n55: \n56: **New Files:**\n57: - `src/editable/mod.rs` - Module exports\n58: - `src/editable/buffer.rs` - TextBuffer traits and implementations\n59: - `src/editable/cursor.rs` - Position and Cursor types\n60: - `src/editable/selection.rs` - Selection operations\n61: - `src/editable/history.rs` - EditOperation and EditHistory\n62: - `src/editable/constraints.rs` - EditConstraints\n63: - `src/editable/state.rs` - EditableState implementation\n64: - `src/editable/context.rs` - EditContext enum\n65: - `src/editable/messages.rs` - TextEditMsg and MoveTarget\n66: - `src/update/text_edit.rs` - TextEditMsg routing, application, and editor bridge\n67: - `src/view/text_field.rs` - TextFieldRenderer\n68: \n69: ---\n70: \n71: ## v0.3.7 - 2025-12-17\n72: \n73: ### Added - Workspace Management & Focus System\n74: \n75: Complete workspace management with sidebar file tree and comprehensive focus handling:\n76: \n77: **Sidebar Resize:**\n78: - Click-and-drag to resize sidebar width\n79: - ColResize cursor shown on hover over resize border\n80: - `SidebarResizeState` tracks drag operation\n81: - Width persists in logical pixels for DPI-independence\n82: \n83: **File Tree Keyboard Navigation:**\n84: - Arrow Up/Down to navigate between items\n85: - Arrow Right expands folders or moves to next item\n86: - Arrow Left collapses folders or jumps to parent folder (standard file tree behavior)\n87: - Enter opens files or toggles folders\n88: - Space toggles folder expansion\n89: - Escape returns focus to editor\n90: \n91: **Workspace Root Display:**\n92: - Workspace root folder is now displayed as the first item in the file tree\n93: - Root folder is auto-expanded when workspace opens\n94: - Root path is canonicalized to ensure proper display name (fixes \".\" showing as empty)\n95: - Folder expand/collapse indicators: `-` for expanded, `+` for collapsed\n96: \n97: **Focus Management System:**\n98: - New `FocusTarget` enum: `Editor`, `Sidebar`, `Modal`\n99: - Explicit focus tracking in `UiState.focus`\n100: - Click on sidebar transfers focus to sidebar\n101: - Click outside sidebar returns focus to editor\n102: - Modals automatically capture/release focus on open/close\n103: - Hiding sidebar while focused returns focus to editor\n104: - `KeyContext.sidebar_focused` and `editor_focused` now reflect actual focus state\n105: \n106: **Global Shortcuts:**\n107: - Command palette (Cmd+Shift+A), Save (Cmd+S), Quit (Cmd+Q), and other global shortcuts now work regardless of focus state\n108: - New `Command::is_global()` method identifies shortcuts that bypass focus-based input routing\n109: - Global commands include: ToggleCommandPalette, ToggleGotoLine, ToggleFindReplace, ToggleSidebar, Quit, SaveFile, NewTab, CloseTab\n110: \n111: **Cursor Icon Cleanup:**\n112: - I-beam cursor only appears over editable text areas\n113: - Default pointer for sidebar, tab bars, status bar, modals, gutter\n114: - ColResize/RowResize for splitters and sidebar resize border\n115: \n116: **Keyboard Routing Fixes:**\n117: - CSV cell editing now properly bypasses keymap (arrow keys work in cell editor)\n118: - Added `AppModel::is_csv_editing()` helper method for consistent checking\n119: \n120: **New Commands:**\n121: - `FileTreeSelectPrevious`, `FileTreeSelectNext`\n122: - `FileTreeOpenOrToggle`, `FileTreeRefresh`\n123: \n124: **New Messages:**\n125: - `WorkspaceMsg::OpenOrToggle` - opens file or toggles folder\n126: - `WorkspaceMsg::SelectParent` - navigate to parent folder\n127: \n128: ---\n129: \n130: ## v0.3.6 - 2025-12-16\n131: \n132: ### Added - CSV Cell Editing (Phase 2)\n133: \n134: Full cell editing support for CSV mode with document synchronization:\n135: \n136: **Editing:**\n137: - **Enter or typing** starts editing the selected cell\n138: - **Typing replaces** cell content when starting with a character\n139: - **Edit cursor** navigation with Left/Right arrows, Home/End\n140: - **Backspace/Delete** for character deletion\n141: - **Enter confirms** edit and moves to next row\n142: - **Tab confirms** edit and moves to next cell\n143: - **Escape cancels** edit, restoring original value\n144: \n145: **Document Sync:**\n146: - Edits update the underlying text buffer in real-time\n147: - Proper CSV escaping for values with delimiters, quotes, or newlines\n148: - Quoted fields are handled correctly (embedded commas don't break parsing)\n149: - File becomes \"modified\" after edits, triggers save prompt\n150: \n151: **New types:**\n152: - `CellEditState` - tracks edit buffer, cursor position, original value\n153: - `CellEdit` - represents a completed edit for sync/undo\n154: \n155: **New messages:**\n156: - `CsvMsg::StartEditing`, `StartEditingWithChar(char)`\n157: - `CsvMsg::ConfirmEdit`, `CancelEdit`\n158: - `CsvMsg::EditInsertChar`, `EditDeleteBackward`, `EditDeleteForward`\n159: - `CsvMsg::EditCursorLeft`, `EditCursorRight`, `EditCursorHome`, `EditCursorEnd`\n160: \n161: ---\n162: \n163: ## v0.3.5 - 2025-12-16\n164: \n165: ### Added - CSV Viewer Mode (Phase 1)\n166: \n167: Spreadsheet-like view for CSV, TSV, and PSV files with full navigation support:\n168: \n169: **Core Features:**\n170: - **Grid rendering** with row numbers (1, 2, 3...) and column headers (A, B, C...)\n171: - **Cell selection** via mouse click with proper hit-testing\n172: - **Delimiter detection** from file extension or content sniffing\n173: - **Column width auto-calculation** based on content (sampled from first 100 rows)\n174: - **Theme integration** with configurable colors for headers, grid lines, selection\n175: - **Memory-efficient storage** using delimited strings (Tablecruncher pattern)\n176: \n177: **Navigation:**\n178: - Arrow keys move cell selection\n179: - Tab/Shift+Tab for next/previous cell with row wrapping\n180: - Home/End for first/last column in row\n181: - Cmd+Home/End for first/last cell in document\n182: - Page Up/Down jumps by viewport height\n183: - Mouse wheel scrolling (vertical and horizontal)\n184: - Click to select cell\n185: \n186: **Integration:**\n187: - Command palette: \"Toggle CSV View\" command\n188: - Escape exits CSV mode\n189: - Status bar shows CSV mode indicator\n190: - Works with split views\n191: \n192: **New files:**\n193: - `src/csv/` module with `mod.rs`, `model.rs`, `parser.rs`, `render.rs`, `navigation.rs`, `viewport.rs`\n194: - `samples/large_data.csv` - 10,001-line test file\n195: - `make csv` target for testing\n196: \n197: **Messages added:**\n198: - `CsvMsg` enum with Toggle, Move*, NextCell, PrevCell, FirstCell, LastCell, RowStart, RowEnd, PageUp, PageDown, Exit, SelectCell, ScrollVertical, ScrollHorizontal\n199: \n200: **Documentation:** See [docs/feature/csv-editor.md](feature/csv-editor.md)\n201: \n202: ---\n203: \n204: ## v0.3.4 - 2025-12-16\n205: \n206: ### Fixed - HiDPI Display Switching\n207: \n208: Fixed critical issues when switching between displays with different DPI/scale factors (e.g., moving window between Retina and non-Retina monitors):\n209: \n210: - **Surface resize on display change** - The softbuffer Surface is now explicitly resized after creation, fixing incorrect rendering when switching displays\n211: - **Buffer bounds checking in Frame** - Frame::new now validates buffer size matches dimensions, preventing panics during display transitions\n212: - **Dynamic tab bar height** - Tab bar height is now computed from actual glyph metrics (`line_height + padding * 2`) instead of hardcoded values\n213: - **Scaled metrics throughout** - Model's `resize()` and `set_char_width()` now use properly scaled metrics for viewport calculations\n214: \n215: ### Changed\n216: \n217: - `ScaleFactorChanged` now triggers both `ReinitializeRenderer` and `Redraw` commands to ensure immediate visual update\n218: - `reinit_renderer` recomputes tab bar height and viewport geometry after font metrics change\n219: - Viewport visible lines calculation now accounts for tab bar height\n220: \n221: ### Technical Details\n222: \n223: - `Renderer::with_scale_factor` explicitly calls `surface.resize()` after creation\n224: - `Frame::new` adjusts height if buffer is smaller than expected (`width * height`)\n225: - `recompute_tab_bar_height_from_line_height()` added to AppModel for font-metric-based sizing\n226: - `group_content_rect_scaled()` now used in rendering for DPI-aware content areas\n227: \n228: ---\n229: \n230: ## v0.3.3 - 2025-12-15\n231: \n232: ### Added - Phase 3-5 Languages for Syntax Highlighting\n233: \n234: Added 11 new languages to syntax highlighting, completing the roadmap phases 3-5:\n235: \n236: **Phase 3 (Priority):**\n237: - **TypeScript** - `tree-sitter-typescript` v0.23 with custom queries\n238: - **TSX** - Shares queries with TypeScript, separate parser\n239: - **JSON** - `tree-sitter-json` v0.24 with custom queries\n240: - **TOML** - `tree-sitter-toml-ng` v0.7 with custom queries\n241: \n242: **Phase 4 (Common):**\n243: - **Python** - `tree-sitter-python` v0.25 using built-in highlights query\n244: - **Go** - `tree-sitter-go` v0.25 using built-in highlights query\n245: - **PHP** - `tree-sitter-php` v0.24 using built-in highlights query\n246: \n247: **Phase 5 (Extended):**\n248: - **C** - `tree-sitter-c` v0.24 using built-in highlights query\n249: - **C++** - `tree-sitter-cpp` v0.23 using built-in highlights query\n250: - **Java** - `tree-sitter-java` v0.23 using built-in highlights query\n251: - **Bash** - `tree-sitter-bash` v0.25 using built-in highlights query\n252: \n253: **Total languages now supported: 17** (PlainText excluded)\n254: \n255: ### Changed\n256: \n257: - Upgraded `tree-sitter` from 0.24 to 0.25 for ABI compatibility with newer grammars\n258: - `LanguageId` enum now includes all 17 language variants\n259: - `from_extension()` recognizes extended file extensions (.tsx, .mts, .cts, .pyw, etc.)\n260: - `from_path()` recognizes special filenames (Makefile, Dockerfile, .bashrc, etc.)\n261: \n262: ### Technical Details\n263: \n264: - Custom queries created for TypeScript, JSON, TOML (in `queries/` directory)\n265: - Phase 4-5 languages use built-in `HIGHLIGHTS_QUERY` or `HIGHLIGHT_QUERY` constants\n266: - Query compilation tests added for all 17 languages\n267: - Parsing tests added for all new languages\n268: - 671 total tests passing\n269: \n270: ---\n271: \n272: ## v0.3.2 - 2025-12-15\n273: \n274: ### Added - Incremental Parsing with tree.edit()\n275: \n276: Implemented proper incremental parsing for significantly faster syntax highlighting on edits:\n277: \n278: **Tree Caching:**\n279: - `ParserState` now caches parsed trees per document in `doc_cache: HashMap<DocumentId, DocParseState>`\n280: - Each `DocParseState` stores the tree, source text, and language\n281: - Cache enables incremental parsing on subsequent edits\n282: \n283: **Edit Diffing:**\n284: - `compute_incremental_edit()` computes `InputEdit` by diffing old vs new source\n285: - Finds common prefix/suffix to minimize the edited region\n286: - `byte_to_point()` converts byte offsets to tree-sitter `Point` (row, column)\n287: \n288: **Incremental Parse Flow:**\n289: 1. On edit, diff cached source against new source\n290: 2. Call `tree.edit(&input_edit)` on cached tree\n291: 3. Pass edited tree to `parser.parse(source, Some(&old_tree))`\n292: 4. Tree-sitter reuses unchanged nodes, only re-parsing the changed region\n293: \n294: **Performance Results:**\n295: | File Size | Full Reparse | Incremental |\n296: |-----------|--------------|-------------|\n297: | 100 lines | 67µs | 68µs |\n298: | 500 lines | 330µs | 356µs |\n299: | 1000 lines | 660µs | 728µs |\n300: | 5000 lines | 3.4ms | 3.6ms |\n301: \n302: *Note: Incremental is similar speed due to highlight extraction dominating; benefit increases for larger files.*\n303: \n304: **New Benchmark Suite** (`benches/syntax.rs`):\n305: - `parse_sample` - Parse small samples for each language\n306: - `parse_only_sample` - Isolated parse time (pre-initialized parser)\n307: - `parse_only_large_rust` - Large file scaling (100-10000 lines)\n308: - `incremental_parse_small_edit` - Incremental with append\n309: - `incremental_parse_middle_edit` - Incremental with mid-file edit\n310: - `full_reparse_comparison` - Fresh parser baseline\n311: \n312: **New Makefile Target:**\n313: - `make bench-syntax` - Run syntax highlighting benchmarks\n314: \n315: ### Changed\n316: \n317: - `ParserState` now includes `doc_cache` for tree caching\n318: - `parse_and_highlight()` uses cached trees for incremental parsing\n319: - Added `clear_doc_cache()` method for document cleanup\n320: \n321: ---\n322: \n323: ## v0.3.1 - 2025-12-15\n324: \n325: ### Fixed - Syntax Highlighting Bugs\n326: \n327: Critical fixes for the syntax highlighting system:\n328: \n329: **Tree-sitter Incremental Parsing Bug:**\n330: - Fixed misaligned highlights after document edits (e.g., pressing Enter)\n331: - Root cause: Passing old cached tree to tree-sitter without calling `tree.edit()`\n332: - Tree-sitter incorrectly reused nodes from stale tree, producing wrong line/column positions\n333: - Fix: Always do full reparse by passing `None` instead of cached tree\n334: - Removed unused tree caching from `ParserState` until proper incremental parsing is implemented\n335: \n336: **Flash of Unstyled Text (FOUC):**\n337: - Fixed jarring unstyled flash during syntax re-parsing\n338: - Old highlights are now preserved until new ones arrive\n339: - Revision checks ensure only matching highlights are applied\n340: - Reduced debounce from 50ms to 30ms for snappier updates\n341: \n342: **Tab Expansion in Highlighting:**\n343: - Fixed highlight token columns not accounting for tab expansion\n344: - Token character columns are now converted to visual columns using `char_col_to_visual_col()`\n345: \n346: ### Changed\n347: \n348: - `SYNTAX_DEBOUNCE_MS` reduced from 50ms to 30ms\n349: - `ParserState` no longer caches syntax trees (simplified until incremental parsing)\n350: \n351: ---\n352: \n353: ## v0.3.0 - 2025-12-15\n354: \n355: ### Added - Benchmark Suite Improvements\n356: \n357: Comprehensive audit and improvement of the benchmark suite:\n358: \n359: **Phase 1: Fixed Inaccurate Benchmarks**\n360: - **Rewrote `glyph_cache.rs`** — Now uses actual fontdue rasterization instead of fictional patterns\n361:   - `glyph_rasterize` tests real character rasterization at various font sizes\n362:   - `glyph_cache_realistic_paragraph` tests actual cache hit/miss patterns\n363:   - `glyph_cache_code_sample` tests with code-like content\n364:   - `font_metrics_extraction` tests line width measurement\n365: - **Created `token::rendering::blend_pixel_u8`** — Shared blend function in lib.rs\n366: - **Updated `rendering.rs` and `support.rs`** — Use shared blend function instead of duplicated code\n367: \n368: **Phase 2: Added Missing Benchmarks**\n369: - **Multi-cursor benchmarks in `main_loop.rs`:**\n370:   - `multi_cursor_setup` (10, 100, 500 cursors)\n371:   - `multi_cursor_insert_char`, `multi_cursor_delete`, `multi_cursor_move_down`\n372:   - `multi_cursor_select_word`, `multi_cursor_add_cursor_above_below`\n373: - **Large file scaling in `rope_operations.rs`** (100k, 500k, 1M lines):\n374:   - `insert_middle_large_file`, `insert_start_large_file`, `insert_end_large_file`\n375:   - `delete_middle_large_file`, `navigate_large_file`\n376:   - Syntax Highlighting MVP\n394: \n395: Tree-sitter based syntax highlighting with async background parsing:\n396: \n397: **New `src/syntax/` module:**\n398: - `highlights.rs` - `HighlightToken`, `LineHighlights`, `SyntaxHighlights` data structures\n399: - `languages.rs` - `LanguageId` enum with extension-based language detection\n400: - `worker.rs` - `SyntaxWorker` with background thread, mpsc channels, debouncing\n401: \n402: **Features:**\n403: - **Async parsing** - Background worker thread prevents UI blocking\n404: - **Debouncing** - 50ms timer prevents parsing on every keystroke\n405: - **Revision tracking** - Staleness checks discard outdated parse results\n406: - **Phase 1 languages** - YAML, Markdown, Rust support\n407: - **Theme integration** - `SyntaxTheme` struct in theme.rs with VS Code-like default colors\n408: - **Auto-trigger** - Parsing on document load and content changes\n409: \n410: **New messages:**\n411: - `SyntaxMsg::ParseCompleted` - Delivers highlights from background thread\n412: - `Cmd::ParseSyntax` - Triggers async syntax parsing\n413: \n414: **Dependencies added:**\n415: - `tree-sitter = \"0.24\"`\n416: - `tree-sitter-yaml = \"0.6\"`\n417: - `tree-sitter-md = \"0.3\"`\n418: - `tree-sitter-rust = \"0.23\"`\n419: \n420: ---\n421: \n422: ### Added - CLI Arguments with clap\n423: \n424: Full command-line argument parsing using clap:\n425: \n426: - **`merge_overlapping_selections()`**: New method in `EditorState` that merges overlapping or touching selections into single selections, maintaining cursor/selection invariants\n879: - **`SelectWord`**: Now operates on ALL cursors, selecting word at each position, then merging overlaps\n880: - **`SelectLine`**: Now operates on ALL cursors, selecting line at each position, then merging overlaps\n881: - **`SelectAll`**: Properly collapses to single cursor + single full-document selection\n882: - **`ExtendSelectionToPosition`**: Collapses multi-cursor first, then extends from primary cursor\n883: - **`word_under_cursor_at(doc, idx)`**: New helper refactored from `word_under_cursor()` for per-cursor word detection\n884: \n885: #### Tests Added\n886: \n887: - `SplitFocused(SplitDirection)` - split current group\n974: - `SplitGroup { group_id, direction }` - split specific group\n975: - `CloseGroup`, `CloseFocusedGroup` - close with layout cleanup\n976: - `FocusGroup`, `FocusNextGroup`, `FocusPrevGroup` - The process involved using the `amp threads share` command with the `--visibility public` flag, followed by verification steps.

### Commands Used for Sharing Threads Publicly

**Initial command structure check:**

```bash
amp t share --help
```

**Output Snippet showing options:**

```
Usage: amp threads share [options] <threadId>

Change thread visibility (private, public, unlisted, workspace, group) or share with Amp support for debugging. Use --visibility to change who can access the thread, or --support to share with the Amp team for troubleshooting.

Options:

  --visibility <visibility>
      Set thread visibility (private, public, unlisted, workspace, group)
  --support [message]
      Share thread with Amp support for debugging
... (Global options omitted) ...
```

**Specific commands executed to make threads public:**

```bash
amp t share --visibility public T-019b2ca6-720c-759f-896d-29eb7d047b2e
```

**Successful output confirmation:**

```
{ "output": "✓ Thread T-019b2ca6-720c-759f-896d-29eb7d047b2e visibility changed to public.\n", "exitCode": 0 }
```

**Batch command example for older threads:**

```bash
for thread_id in T-7b92a860-a2f7-4397-985c-73b2fa3e9582 T-750a0e44-2302-4b5e-8cdc-70b14c3f7930 T-ce8edd72-f084-4fba-8c86-276df333de96 T-de4eaf86-9b34-489a-b6c8-e5c0154f1aff T-519a8c9d-b94f-45e5-98e0-5bfc34c77cbf T-60e201bf-322a-4190-8671-3afe9ad7500e; do
  amp t share --visibility public "$thread_id" 2>/dev/null || echo "Skipped $thread_id"
done
```

The comprehensive plan is at docs/feature/unified-text-editing-final.md (~3,700 lines).\n\nI just completed Phase 1 - creating the src/editable/ module foundation:\n- TextBuffer/TextBufferMut traits abstracting over Rope and String backends\n- StringBuffer and RopeBuffer implementations\n- Position, Cursor, Selection structs using line/column (not byte offsets)\n- EditableState<B> as the main abstraction with all editing operations (movement, selection, insert, delete, word operations, undo/redo)\n- EditConstraints for context-specific restrictions (single-line, numeric filter, max length, etc.)\n- EditHistory for undo/redo\n- TextEditMsg enum with MoveTarget for cursor movement\n- EditContext enum for routing (Editor, CommandPalette, GotoLine, FindQuery, ReplaceQuery, CsvCell)\n- 52 unit tests all passing, clippy clean\n\nPhase 2 requires:\n- Add Msg::TextEdit(EditContext, TextEditMsg) variant to src/messages.rs\n- Create src/update/text_edit.rs with update_text_edit() unified handler\n- Wire up dispatch in src/update/mod.rs to route TextEdit messages\n- The plan specifies the handler should match on context to find the appropriate EditableState and apply the message\n\nKey patterns:\n- Use from_text() not from_str() for buffer constructors (clippy requirement)\n- Word boundary detection uses existing CharType from src/util/text.rs\n- Cursor uses line/column to match existing EditorState model\n- EditContext::constraints() returns appropriate EditConstraints for each context\n\nThe plan includes complete Rust implementations for all types - reference docs/feature/unified-text-editing-final.md sections 5.1-5.3 for Phase 2 details.\n\nproceed to phase 2",
          "type": "text"
        }
      ]
    },

      "title": "Implementing unified text editing architecture",
      "labels": [],
      "created": 1766144144822,

      "creatorUserID": "[redacted-user-id]",
      "viewerHasStarred": false,
      "firstUserMessageContent": [
        {
          "text": "Continuing work from thread T-019b3643-79af-7573-bb69-18dac832eb94. When you lack specific information you can use read_thread to get it.\n\n@docs/feature/unified-text-editing-final.md @src/model/editor.rs @src/model/document.rs @src/model/ui.rs @src/csv/model.rs @src/update/editor.rs @src/update/document.rs @src/util/text.rs\n\nOther relevant files: src/update/ui.rs, src/update/csv.rs, src/runtime/input.rs, src/view/mod.rs, src/messages.rs\n\nI created a comprehensive plan for unifying text editing across all input areas in the editor. The plan is at docs/feature/unified-text-editing-final.md (~3,700 lines).\n\nThe problem: 5 separate text editing implementations with duplicated logic and inconsistent behavior:\n- Main Editor (Rope buffer, full features)\n- Command Palette (String, end-only cursor, no navigation)\n- Go-to-Line (String, end-only cursor)\n- Find/Replace (String, end-only cursor)\n- CSV Cell Editor (String + byte offset cursor, limited features)\n\nThe solution creates a new src/editable/ module with:\n- TextBuffer and TextBufferMut traits abstracting over Rope and String backends\n- RopeBuffer and StringBuffer implementations\n- Position, Cursor, Selection structs using line/column (not byte offsets)\n- EditableState<B> as the main abstraction with all editing operations\n- EditConstraints for context-specific restrictions (single-line, numeric filter, etc.)\n- EditHistory for undo/redo\n- TextEditMsg enum with MoveTarget for cursor movement\n- EditContext enum for routing (Editor, CommandPalette, GotoLine, FindQuery, ReplaceQuery, CsvCell)\n\nKey architectural decisions:\n- Split TextBuffer/TextBufferMut traits (read-only for rendering)\n- Line/column cursors match existing EditorState model\n- MoveTarget enum reduces message duplication (Move vs MoveWithSelection share targets)\n- EditContext makes message routing explicit\n- Bridge migration for main editor (map TextEditMsg to existing EditorMsg/DocumentMsg first)\n\nImplementation order (8 milestones over 6 weeks):\n1. Foundation - create src/editable/ module with all types\n2. Message System - add Msg::TextEdit(EditContext, TextEditMsg)\n3. CSV Cell Migration - simplest context, isolated\n4. Modal Migration - command palette, go-to-line, find/replace\n5. Rendering - TextFieldRenderer with TextFieldContent trait\n6. Main Editor Bridge - map TextEditMsg to existing messages\n7. Main Editor Migration - incrementally replace internals\n8. Cleanup - remove deprecated code\n\nThe plan includes complete Rust implementations for all structs, keyboard shortcut mappings for macOS/Windows/Linux, message type mappings from old to new, and manual testing checklists.\n\nExisting word boundary logic is in src/util/text.rs with CharType enum - this should be reused.\n\nnext we will implement the new plan @docs/feature/unified-text-editing-final.md ",
          "type": "text"
        }
      ]
    },

      "title": "Sidebar arrow key navigation not working after click",
      "labels": [],
      "created": 1765980664343,
      "starCount": 0,
      "updatedAt": "2025-12-17T14:51:00.101Z",
      "firstSyncAt": "2025-12-17T14:11:11.383Z",
      "messageCount": 234,
      "summaryStats": {
        "diffStats": {
          "added": 261,
          "changed": 34,
          "deleted": 84
        },
        "toolUsage": {
          "Bash": 28,
          "Grep": 15,
          "Read": 34,
          "edit_file": 28,
          "todo_write": 8
        },
        "messageCount": 234,
        "humanMessageCount": 10
      },
      "creatorUserID": "[redacted-user-id]",
      "viewerHasStarred": false,
      "firstUserMessageContent": [
        {
          "text": "Continuing work from thread T-019b2c8a-b043-70ee-b7c6-5576369d38f3. When you lack specific information you can use read_thread to get it.\n\n@src/runtime/input.rs @src/runtime/app.rs @src/model/ui.rs @src/update/workspace.rs @src/messages.rs\n\nI just implemented a comprehensive focus management system for the sidebar file tree:\n\n- I added a FocusTarget enum (Editor, Sidebar, Modal) in src/model/ui.rs to track which UI region has keyboard focus\n- I added HoverRegion enum to track where the mouse is hovering (used for scroll routing)\n- I added focus_editor(), focus_sidebar(), focus_modal() helper methods to UiState for consistent focus management\n- I fixed sidebar keyboard focus gating in src/runtime/input.rs so keys don't fall through to editor when sidebar is focused\n\nThe issue now is that clicking on a sidebar item and pressing arrow keys doesn't navigate the sidebar. The problem is likely in one of these areas:\n\n1. In src/runtime/app.rs, clicking in the sidebar should call focus_sidebar() - I verified this is happening around line 673\n2. In src/runtime/input.rs, the is_sidebar_focused() function checks if model.ui.focus == FocusTarget::Sidebar\n3. The handle_sidebar_key() function in src/runtime/input.rs handles Arrow Up/Down by sending WorkspaceMsg::SelectPrevious/SelectNext\n\nThe keyboard routing flow is:\n- handle_key() checks is_sidebar_focused() at line 59\n- If true, it calls handle_sidebar_key() which handles arrow keys\n- handle_sidebar_key returns Some(cmd) for handled keys or None for unhandled\n\nThe focus state should be set when clicking in sidebar (focus_sidebar() called), but keyboard events may not be reaching handle_sidebar_key. I added trace logging to focus changes - running with RUST_LOG=trace should show focus transitions.\n\nKey files for debugging:\n- src/runtime/input.rs - keyboard routing and is_sidebar_focused()\n- src/runtime/app.rs - mouse click handling that sets focus\n- src/model/ui.rs - FocusTarget enum and focus helper methods\n- src/update/workspace.rs - SelectPrevious/SelectNext handlers\n\nscrolling works now, however clicking on an item and pressing arrow keys do not navigate the sidebar areas",
          "type": "text"
        }
      ]
    },

          "text": "Continuing work from thread T-019b2c40-3b31-764a-a3c9-31369108ef99. When you lack specific information you can use read_thread to get it.\n\n@src/model/ui.rs @src/runtime/app.rs @src/runtime/input.rs @src/keymap/context.rs @src/update/workspace.rs @src/model/mod.rs @src/model/workspace.rs @src/view/mod.rs @src/view/geometry.rs\n\nI just implemented an explicit focus management system to control keyboard input routing between different UI areas.\nI introduced a `FocusTarget` enum (`Editor`, `Sidebar`, `Modal`) in src/model/ui.rs and added a `focus` field to `UiState` to track the currently focused area.\nI updated `UiState::open_modal()` and `UiState::close_modal()` to automatically set/reset focus to `Modal` or `Editor`.\nI modified `get_key_context()` in src/runtime/app.rs to derive `editor_focused` and `sidebar_focused` from the new `UiState.focus` field, ensuring keybindings are context-aware based on explicit focus.\nI updated `is_sidebar_focused()` in src/runtime/input.rs to check `UiState.focus` for routing sidebar-specific keyboard events.\nI added logic to src/runtime/app.rs to transfer focus to `Sidebar` on any mouse click within the sidebar area, and to `Editor` on clicks outside the sidebar (but not in a modal).\nI modified the `WorkspaceMsg::ToggleSidebar` handler in src/update/workspace.rs to return focus to `Editor` if the sidebar is hidden while it was focused.\nI changed the `Escape` key handler in src/runtime/input.rs, when the sidebar is focused, to explicitly set focus to `Editor`.\nI updated the CHANGELOG, ROADMAP, and feature documentation (`docs/feature/workspace-management.md`) to reflect these changes.\n\nHowever, you mentioned that \"focus handling, nor scrolling in sidebar is working\". This indicates that despite my recent changes, there are still issues with how focus is managed and how scrolling behaves in the sidebar. This might require a deeper review of how mouse events, keyboard events, and rendering interact with the sidebar's state and layout.\n\nThe core architecture follows an Elm-style (Model -> Message -> Update -> View) with context-aware keybindings and DPI-aware scaling.\nThe sidebar width is calculated via `workspace.sidebar_width(scale_factor)` and renders from x=0.\nThe cursor icon handling was also recently cleaned up to be more consistent, with `CursorIcon::Text` only appearing over editor areas, `CursorIcon::Default` for most UI elements, and `CursorIcon::ColResize/RowResize` for resize handles.\n\nfocus handling, nor scrolling in sidebar is working, consult oracle to cleanup this behjaviour properly, review entire codebase and suggest improvements/refactorings to ensure focus handling, scroll targets and hovering regions are properly working as expected",

When you lack specific information you can use read_thread to get it.\n\n@src/runtime/app.rs @src/view/mod.rs @src/view/geometry.rs @src/model/workspace.rs\n\nI am implementing workspace management for a Rust text editor with an Elm-style architecture (messages, update handlers, commands).\n\nRecent work completed:\n- Fixed editor area hit testing coordinate offset issue -       "created": 1765968136362,

      "firstUserMessageContent": [
        {
          "text": "Continuing work from thread T-019b2bc5-dd85-7066-a46c-2e14bb039078. When you lack specific information you can use read_thread to get it.\n\n@src/runtime/app.rs @src/view/geometry.rs @src/view/mod.rs @src/view/frame.rs @src/model/mod.rs @src/model/editor_area.rs\n\nI am implementing workspace management for a Rust text editor with an Elm-style architecture (messages, update handlers, commands).\n\nCurrent issue: Editor area hit testing is not offset by the sidebar width. Clicks in the editor area use absolute window coordinates instead of being converted to local coordinates relative to the editor region. This causes clicks to be misaligned when the sidebar is visible.\n\nWhat I discovered about the coordinate system:\n- Mouse coordinates from winit are in physical pixels (already scaled)\n- All metrics (file_tree_row_height, tab_bar_height, etc.) are pre-scaled by scale_factor in ScaledMetrics\n- The sidebar renders from x=0, y=0 of the window\n- The editor area starts at x=sidebar_width, y=0\n- The view/geometry.rs module has pixel_to_cursor() which converts pixel coords to document line/column, but it doesn't account for sidebar offset\n- Frame in view/frame.rs provides drawing primitives but doesn't track region offsets\n\nThe architectural goal: Create a \"region\" abstraction where each render area (sidebar, modal, editor group, etc.) can convert clicks to local coordinates. This would:\n- Store the region's position within the parent/window\n- Convert absolute window coordinates to region-local coordinates\n- Allow hit testing to work correctly regardless of layout changes\n\nRelevant patterns:\n- Rendering uses Frame for drawing primitives\n- Editor groups have their own tab bars and content areas\n- pixel_to_cursor_in_group() exists in geometry.rs but takes group_rect as parameter\n- The sidebar hit testing I fixed works correctly because it checks if x < sidebar_width first\n\nKey files for coordinate handling:\n- src/view/geometry.rs - pixel_to_cursor() and related functions\n- src/view/frame.rs - Frame struct for drawing\n- src/runtime/app.rs - mouse event handling\n- src/view/mod.rs - Renderer and render functions\n\nThe fix likely needs to:\n1. Pass sidebar_width to mouse handling code in app.rs\n2. Offset mouse coordinates by sidebar_width before calling pixel_to_cursor\n3. Or create a proper region/viewport abstraction that handles this automatically\n\nthere is a remaining bug realted to hit testing inside editor areas, they are not offset by the size of the sidebar, meaning we are operating on absolute window positioning, but we need each seperate render-area (sidebar, modal, textarea, split container etc to convert clicks inside of it to local positions, currently clicking in the editorarea still thinks that the editorarea is positioned relative to top-left of window, but when sidebar is there, this has to change, we need to investigate a proper archeitectual fix so that these kinds of issues are easier to do, this was what the viewprot geometry and frame stuff was meant to do for us, create a \"region\" where positions could use logical offsets and all of these things would be correclty computed and offset based on the region positioning , consult oracle, review the ui rendering code, use search and librarian subagents to figure out a comprehensive solution to this issue and implement the fix throughout all rendering code",
          "type": "text"
        }
      ]
    },

I integrated the `Workspace` struct into `AppModel` in src/model/mod.rs, replacing the old `workspace_root` field.\n- Phase 3: I added the `WorkspaceMsg` enum to src/messages.rs and created the `update_workspace()` handler in src/update/workspace.rs, which I then integrated into the main update loop in src/update/mod.rs. This handles toggling the sidebar, expanding/collapsing folders, and selecting items.\n- Phase 4: I added a `SidebarTheme` struct to src/theme.rs and updated the `Theme::from_data` and `Theme::default_dark` methods to include sidebar colors. I bound Cmd+1 to toggle the sidebar and Cmd+Shift+R to reveal the active file in the sidebar in keymap.yaml.\n- I updated the Makefile to include a `make workspace` command, which opens the `samples/syntax` directory as a workspace.\n\nI followed the Elm-like architecture with messages, update handlers, and commands. The codebase uses `ScaledMetrics` for DPI scaling and a YAML-based theme system.\n\nThe remaining tasks for workspace management are:\n- Implement sidebar resize drag.\n- Update cursor on resize hover.\n- Implement arrow key navigation in the file tree.\n- Implement expand/collapse with Enter/arrows.\n- Implement file system watching (Phase 7).\n- Integrate preview tabs (Phase 8).\n\nfirst off we have a hit test offset bug, the sidebar looks to be visually shifted down 1 lines worth and clicks hits seem to be offset down by a line row, investigate this thuroughly by consulting oracle, text in sidebar can also overflow the sidebar container, it should be clipped/truncated if too long, clicking a file should require a double click to open, same with opening folders in sidebar (double click to open/close), for the collapse/oppen icon we can use a chevron instead, or alternatively \"-\" and \">\" text chars for simplicity, also remember to update theme.yaml with sidebar colors etc",

When you lack specific information you can use read_thread to get it.\n\n@src/csv/model.rs @src/csv/parser.rs @src/messages.rs @src/update/csv.rs @src/update/mod.rs @src/runtime/input.rs @src/view/mod.rs @docs/feature/csv-editor.md @docs/CHANGELOG.md\n\nI just completed Phase 2 of the CSV Viewer feature, which is Cell Editing.\n\nI implemented the following capabilities:\n- Cell editing: I can now press Enter or start typing to edit the selected cell.\n- Edit buffer management: I added a CellEditState struct to track the edit buffer, cursor position, and original value.\n- Cursor navigation within cells: I can use Left/Right arrow keys, Home, and End to move the cursor inside the cell editor.\n- Character input: I can insert characters, delete backward with Backspace, and delete forward with Delete.\n- Confirm/Cancel edit: Enter confirms the edit and moves to the next row (or up if I press the Up arrow while editing), Tab confirms and moves to the next cell, and Escape cancels the edit.\n- Document synchronization: When an edit is confirmed, I update the underlying document buffer with the new cell value, ensuring proper CSV escaping for values containing delimiters, quotes, or newlines.\n\nKey implementation details and patterns I used:\n- I added CellEditState and CellEdit structs to src/csv/model.rs.\n- I added editing-related methods to the CsvState struct in src/csv/model.rs.\n- I introduced new CsvMsg variants in src/messages.rs for all editing actions.\n- I implemented the escape_csv_value function in src/csv/parser.rs to handle CSV escaping according to RFC 4180.\n- I updated src/update/csv.rs to handle all the new CsvMsg variants and to manage the document synchronization logic using find_row_byte_range and find_field_byte_range.\n- I refined the input routing in src/update/mod.rs and src/runtime/input.rs. The system now checks if I am in CSV mode AND if I am currently editing a cell to correctly route keyboard input. This was crucial for ensuring Enter commits the edit and arrow keys move the cursor within the cell rather than moving the cell selection.\n- I added the rendering logic for the cell editor overlay and cursor in src/view/mod.rs.\n- I updated the documentation in docs/feature/csv-editor.md and docs/CHANGELOG.md to reflect the completion of Phase 2.\n\nAll tests are passing, and the feature is fully integrated.\n\nlets update the docs, changelog roadmap etc, and tag a new release",

        }
      ]

Building from source\n17: \n18: To build Token from source, you will need the [Rust toolchain](https://rustup.rs/) installed.\n19: The `makefile setup` takes care of installing any other dependencies.\n20: \n21: ```shell\n22: git clone https://github.com/HelgeSverre/token\n23: cd token\n24: \n25: # Install dependencies (and misc tools)\n26: make setup\n27: \n28: # Build and run with Makefile\n29: make dev\n30: \n31: # Or run seperately\n32: make build\n33: make run\n34: \n35: # Or build and run manually with cargo,\n36: # like a regular boring person...\n37: cargo build --release\n38: cargo run\n39: ```\n40: \n41: ## Commands\n42: \n43: List them with `make help`\n44: \n45: ### Build & Run\n46: \n47: | Command        | Description                                      |\n48: | -------------- | ------------------------------------------------ |\n49: | `make build`   | Build debug binary                               |\n50: | `make release` | Build optimized release binary                   |\n51: | `make run`     | Run with default sample file (indentation.txt)   |\n52: | `make dev`     | Run debug build (faster compile, slower runtime) |\n53: | `make csv`     | Run with large CSV file (tests CSV viewer)       |\n54: | `make clean`   | Remove build artifacts                           |\n55: | `make fmt`     | Format Rust code and markdown files              |\n56: \n57: ### Testing\n58: \n59: | Command             | Description                            |\n60: | ------------------- | -------------------------------------- |\n61: | `make test`         | Run all tests                          |\n62: | `make test-verbose` | Run tests with output                  |\n63: | `make test-fast`    | Run tests faster using `cargo nextest` |\n64: \n65: ### Features\n66: \n67: | Feature                  | Description                                                                  |\n68: | ------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n69: | Multi-cursor editing     | Option+Click to add cursors, Option+Option+Arrow to add above/below          |\n70: | Split views              | Horizontal and vertical splits with independent viewports                    |\n71: | Syntax highlighting      | Tree-sitter based, 17 languages supported                                    |\n72: | **CSV Viewer**           | Spreadsheet view for CSV/TSV/PSV files (Command Palette → \"Toggle CSV View\") |\n73: | Configurable keybindings | YAML-based keymap with context-aware bindings                                |\n74: | Themes                   | Dark and light themes with full customization                                |\n75: \n76: ---\n77: \n78: ## The Hidden Complexity of Text Editors\n79: \n80: You use a text editor every day. But do you actually understand what it's doing?\n81: \n82: On the surface, an editor is \"just\" text on a screen with a blinking cursor. Underneath, it's a small physics engine for\n83: glyphs, pixels, and input events—quietly solving dozens of problems every time you press a key.\n84: \n85: Consider a few things you rely on constantly but probably don't think about:\n86: \n87: - **Cursor + selection choreography** — Hold Shift and press arrow keys: the cursor moves, the selection grows or\n88:   shrinks, and the viewport sometimes nudges just enough to keep everything visible. Double-click a word, then\n89:   Shift+Click somewhere else. The rules feel obvious—until you try to implement them.\n90: \n91: - **Keyboard modifier edge cases** — What's the \"right\" behavior for Ctrl+Left on a line with emoji, snake_case, and\n92:   camelCase? What happens to your selection when you hit Cmd+Z, then Cmd+Shift+Z, then type? When should Alt extend\n93:   selection, Cmd jump by word, or Option+Option+Arrow add a new cursor?\n94: \n95: - **Glyphs that don't behave like characters** — A single \"character\" on screen might be multiple codepoints: combining\n96:   accents, zero-width joiners, ligatures. The cursor should snap to visually sensible positions, not halfway through a\n97:   grapheme cluster. Column count, byte offset, and rendered width all disagree—and the editor must reconcile them.\n98: \n99: - **Viewport scrolling that feels \"natural\"** — Page Down doesn't simply add `viewportHeight` to `scrollY`. It decides\n100:   which line becomes the new top, keeps the cursor in a \"safe zone,\" and handles partial lines at edges. Mouse wheel,\n[... omitted lines 101 to 740 ...]",

{
  "path": "~/code/rust-editor/docs/AMPCODE_THREADS.md",
  "content": "# Ampcode Thread Management\n\nThis document describes how to manage and share Amp threads for this project.\n\n## Thread Visibility\n\nAmp threads can have different visibility levels:\n- `private` - Only visible to you\n- `public` - Visible to anyone with the link\n- `unlisted` - Not listed publicly but accessible via link\n- `workspace` - Visible to workspace members\n- `group` - Visible to specific groups\n\n## Making Threads Public\n\nTo share a thread publicly, use the `amp threads share` command:\n\n```bash\n# Check available options\namp t share --help\n\n# Make a single thread public\namp t share --visibility public <thread_id>\n\n# Example\namp t share --visibility public T-019b369a-6bd2-76de-9d1c-121e3bd78e2c\n```\n\n### Batch Sharing Multiple Threads\n\nTo make multiple threads public at once:\n\n```bash\nfor thread_id in T-xxx T-yyy T-zzz; do\n  amp t share --visibility public \"$thread_id\" 2>/dev/null && echo \"✓ $thread_id\" || echo \"✗ $thread_id\"\ndone\n```\n\n## Finding Threads\n\n### Using find_thread Tool\n\nIn an Amp conversation, use the `find_thread` tool to search for threads:\n\n```\nQuery: \"repo:HelgeSverre/token after:7d\"\n```\n\nSupported filters:\n- `repo:owner/repo` - Filter by repository\n- `after:7d` or `after:2024-01-15` - Threads after date\n- `before:2w` - Threads before date\n- `author:me` - Your threads\n- `file:path/to/file` - Threads that touched a file\n\n### Using CLI\n\n```bash\n# List recent threads\namp t list\n\n# List threads for a specific repo (if supported)\namp t list --repo HelgeSverre/token\n```\n\n## Thread URL Format\n\nPublic threads are accessible at:\n```\nhttps://ampcode.com/threads/<thread_id>\n```\n\nExample:\n```\nhttps://ampcode.com/threads/T-019b369a-6bd2-76de-9d1c-121e3bd78e2c\n```\n\n## Updating README with Thread References\n\nThe README.md includes a section listing development threads. When adding new entries:\n\n1. Find new threads using `find_thread` with `repo:HelgeSverre/token after:<last_update>`\n2. Make them public using `amp t share --visibility public <id>`\n3. Add entries to the thread table in README.md with format:\n   ```markdown\n   | 2025-12-19 | [Thread Title](https://ampcode.com/threads/<id>) | Feature | Brief description |\n   ```\n\n## Verification\n\nTo verify how many threads are linked in README:\n\n```bash\ngrep -c \"ampcode.com/threads\" README.md\n```\n\n## Profile Link\n\nAll public threads are also visible on the author's Amp profile:\n- https://ampcode.com/@helgesverre\n"
}
```

}
